<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## developer

You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.

Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\r\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\r\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\r\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\r\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\r\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **"Joshua"**, students display their real profile names.\r\n\r\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\r\n\r\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\r\n\r\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\r\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\r\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\nAny change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + browser tool, e.g. Playwright or chrome devtools MCP), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Do not report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This has been the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions (repeated "it\'s not working" / "i dont see it" corrections) — treat it as a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n\r\n**Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** That means narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A responsive defect that gets pushed as "fixed" and does not hold costs a second full session — this has already happened (a pushed Home-page fix that did not survive, then 13 screenshots and 8 emulate cycles to actually close it). One matrix pass before the push is cheaper than either.\r\n\r\n## Product rules (must not regress)\r\n\r\n- Foyer (`FoyerSidebar` + `FoyerHeroRing`) is the primary navigation. The Foyer additionally hosts a `GooeyDock` quick-launch row well below the orbital ring; this dock is allowed only on `/foyer`. Do not add docks elsewhere.\r\n- Inbox is real messaging with back-and-forth threads and PDF attachments, scoped 1:1 per student with admin.\r\n- Admin is fully responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Do not expose actions users cannot actually perform — no fake analytics, no dead buttons, no misleading placeholders. Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake data.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email.\r\n\r\n## Known gaps (do not invent answers)\r\n\r\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run them with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\r\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows. Nothing runs `typecheck`, `lint`, or the tests automatically — `.claude/safe-push.sh` is the only gate, and only for typecheck.\r\n- `README.md` is a stub.\r\n- `.claude/settings.json` is set up with a permission allowlist for safe dev work (npm scripts, git reads, MCP GitHub reads) and a denylist for destructive ops. No custom `.claude/agents/`. A `SessionStart` auto-pull hook is wired in `.claude/settings.local.json` (runs `.claude/auto-pull-hook.sh`); Codex mirrors it in `.codex/hooks.json`.\r\n\r\n## Skill / agent / command vault\r\n\r\nThe full skill/agent/command vault lives in the brain root at `~/ai-os/skills/`, `~/ai-os/agents/`, `~/ai-os/commands/` (split into `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees, indexed by `~/ai-os/skills/INDEX.md`). It is inert storage — nothing here auto-loads. To use a vault skill in this project, symlink it into `.claude/skills/` (Codex: `.agents/skills/`). When you create a new skill/agent/command, save it to BOTH this project\'s `.claude/<kind>/` and the vault, so the vault stays the complete catalog.\r\n\r\n## Agent skills\r\n\r\n### Issue tracker\r\n\r\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\r\n\r\n### Triage labels\r\n\r\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\r\n\r\n### Domain docs\r\n\r\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\r\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# Activity Centre\n\nThe home for bespoke, coach-led skill exercises (e.g. Negation Station) that a student is assigned and works through conversationally. Distinct from the question-bank MCQ **Drill** engine: Activities are authored content with their own answer keys, not timed runs over `qid` questions.\n\n## Language\n\n**Activity Centre**:\nThe module that hosts and lists Activities. The student\'s home for assigned skill work that isn\'t question-bank drilling.\n_Avoid_: Activities Centre, Skills Centre, Drill Centre\n\n**Activity**:\nOne bespoke skill exercise — a titled, self-contained set of authored items with an answer key, worked through with a coach (e.g. "Negation Station"). The unit a teacher assigns.\n_Avoid_: Skill drill, drill (reserved for the MCQ engine), station, module, game\n\n**Homepage**:\nJoshua-facing shorthand for an individual Activity\'s intro/start page: the page that shows the Activity title, item count, overview copy, example, and Start action before the student begins the first Question Set.\n_Avoid_: LSAT U landing page, Foyer, Activity Centre list\n\n**Question Set**:\nCodex-facing term for one complete per-question unit inside an Activity: the Question Type, the prompt, the response area, the coach feedback, the credited answer, and the answer key material used to judge the response. In the UI, this should still be labeled "Question N"; when Joshua says "question set," he means this full unit, not the collection of all questions.\n_Avoid_: Question collection, drill set, item bank\n\n**Question Type**:\nThe classification pill inside a Question Set that names what kind of statement or reasoning form the student is working with (e.g. "Universal Statement," "Conditional Statement," "Causal Statement"). This is the canonical term for the label currently shown above the prompt.\n_Avoid_: Category, tag, badge, label\n\n**Drill**:\nReserved term. The existing question-bank engine: a timed run over multiple-choice questions pulled by `qid`. An Activity is NOT a Drill.\n\n**Item**:\nOne prompt within an Activity that the student responds to (e.g. a single statement to negate). Ordered easy→hard as authored.\n_Avoid_: Question (overloaded with the qid question bank), problem, task\n\n**Credited answer**:\nThe author-supplied correct response to an Item, used as ground truth when the coach judges a student\'s response. Some Items carry several acceptable phrasings.\n_Avoid_: Solution, key answer, right answer\n\n**Coach**:\nThe conversational AI tutor that presents Items one at a time, reads the student\'s free-text response, and gives immediate supportive feedback against the credited answer. Built on the existing AI-gateway tutor pattern (`analyze-stem-response`).\nConditional Rephrase is the exception: its constrained `If ..., then ...` responses are judged by an authoritative local parser, so the Activity works without AI credits or a network request.\n_Avoid_: Tutor (reserved for the standalone Tutor surface), bot, grader\n\n**Verdict**:\nThe coach\'s judgement of a single student response: `correct`, `partial`, or `incorrect`. `partial` captures a near-miss the Activity exists to teach (e.g. a polar-opposite instead of a true negation). Drives the assignment score and future weakness analytics.\n_Avoid_: Grade, mark, result\n\n**Activity assignment**:\nA per-student issued copy of an Activity, snapshotting its content at assign time and tracking the student\'s progress/score. Mirrors the homework `assignment` pattern; immutable snapshot once issued.\n_Avoid_: Homework (that\'s the MCQ-set pattern), task\n\n### Read, Recall, Colour\n\nThe four-step vocabulary of the RRC runner, plus the language its grading uses.\nThe source method\'s own words for these steps are listed under `_Avoid_` and are\nnever used in the product.\n\n**Chunk**:\nAn authored cut of a stimulus, roughly a sentence, carrying the Details that fall inside it. A guide for her eye and the unit content is written in, never a boundary she is held to: she reads, recalls and colours in whatever units she likes.\n_Avoid_: Line, segment, piece, part\n\n**Read** (step 1):\nThe silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken.\n_Avoid_: Skim, study\n\n**Retelling** (step 2):\nSaying a chunk back out loud in her own words. **Retelling** is the word we use to each other and in code; the label a student reads is always **Tell it back**.\n_Avoid_: Reading Recall (its former name, which put "Recall" on two different steps), Say it back (the old student label), translate, translation, paraphrase, summary, repeat-back\n\n**Colouring In** (step 3):\nHer spoken reaction to the chunk she just recalled — a thought, doubt, example, or joke that fits that chunk and no other.\n_Avoid_: Quip, comment, annotation, reaction\n\n**The Recall** (step 4):\nThe end of a stimulus, worked with the text gone: she says the whole argument back from memory. Graded on Coverage.\n_Avoid_: Retelling (that is step 2), CLIR, recap, rebound, takeaway, The Read (see below: that is now the other number, not this step\'s former name)\n\n**The Read**:\nThe analytical takeaway, and the second graded number: what the argument is DOING, as opposed to what it says. Authored per stimulus as `the_read`, judged as two flags, `read_given` and `read_matches` ([ADR 0025](../../../docs/adr/0025-the-read-is-the-second-number.md)). It is a dimension of the grading, never a fifth step she is asked to perform out loud.\n\nIt was step 4\'s name until [ADR 0012](../../../docs/adr/0012-the-recall-is-a-recap-from-memory.md) renamed that step to The Recall, and this entry did not exist for the six days between 0012 and 0025, so "The Read" read as simply retired. It is not. On the Report Card, **Your Read** is correct and must not be collapsed into The Recall: they are the reasoning half and the memory half, and they fail differently.\n_Avoid_: Takeaway, the point, the gist, analysis\n\n**the move**:\nWhat a stimulus\'s Read turns on, in the judge\'s and the card\'s shorthand: the loophole, inference or conflict the argument runs on. Always defined on a surface before it is used, because "you found the move" means nothing to a student reading it for the first time.\n_Avoid_: The trick, the gotcha, the key (reserved for the authored answer)\n\n**Detail**:\nOne of the small authored facts a stimulus contains. PT102.S3.Q1 holds 21.\n_Avoid_: Point, fact, bullet, item (reserved for an Activity\'s prompt)\n\n**Coverage**:\nHow many of a stimulus\'s Details she carried, counted across everything she said about it rather than against any one Chunk.\n_Avoid_: Accuracy, completeness, recall rate\n\n**Reciting**:\nSaying a stimulus back close to word-for-word. A failure of the Retelling, not a step of its own: Coverage looks perfect and nothing was understood.\n_Avoid_: Parroting, copying, verbatim recall, memorising\n\n**Listing**:\nDelivering a stimulus as a flat run of facts rather than a story in her own words. The opposite failure to Reciting: Coverage looks perfect and the argument was never held together.\n_Avoid_: Shopping list, itemising, enumerating\n\n**Report Card**:\nWhat a finished section produces: every stimulus graded on its own merits, showing what she missed and what she got perfect, with her own recording playable beside it. One player per stimulus, never per question, because two questions can share a stimulus and she recorded it once.\n_Avoid_: Scorecard (informally taken by the MCQ Drill results screen), Verdict (that is the coach judging live, which this surface never does), results, grade, review\n\n**Clip**:\nOne stimulus\'s worth of her recording, playable on the Report Card. A Clip is cut out of the single unbroken section recording after she finishes; it is never recorded as its own file, because the drill forbids stopping the microphone mid-section.\n_Avoid_: Take, snippet, segment (that is the timing record the Clip is cut from), track\n\n**Attempt**:\nOne complete run of one section by one student. A section may be attempted any number of times, and every Attempt keeps its own date, its own recording and its own Report Card. Nothing is overwritten and nothing is hidden once done.\n_Avoid_: Try, retry, session (reserved for a tutoring session), run\n\n**Ungraded**:\nA stimulus the judge never answered for, because the call to it failed. Distinct from a stimulus she said nothing about, which is a real zero: this one has no measurement at all. It is excluded from Coverage and from every count of her habits, because reporting our outage as her behaviour is worse than reporting nothing.\n_Avoid_: Failed, missing, unscored, zero, error\n\n**Sweep**:\nThe scheduled re-drive of a finished section that never got a grade, or got a partial one. The client asks for grading the moment she stops and does not wait on the answer; the Sweep is what makes that ask safe to lose. A closed tab never costs a Report Card.\n_Avoid_: Retry, cron, job, backfill, reconcile\n\n### The section launch surface\n\nThe page a student meets before a section run, and the parts of it that carry\nnames. Nothing here is a phase of the runner; these are things drawn on one\npage.\n\n**Section Launch**:\nThe page a student lands on before running an RRC section: the method as four Beats, her last few Attempts, and the one control that starts the run. `SectionLaunch` in code, `.sl-` in CSS. It replaces the editorial intro for section mode only; the eight typed Activities keep theirs.\n_Avoid_: The Chamber (the approved mockup\'s name for it, and already a world under `worlds/chamber/`), Homepage (that is an Activity\'s own intro page), intro, splash, lobby, dashboard\n\n**Beat**:\nInternal code term for one of the four method steps shown on Section Launch. It may remain in implementation names, but it is never student-facing. Students see **step**: Read it, Tell it back, Colour it, Recall it.\n_Avoid in student-facing copy_: Beat, beats, phase, tab, card\n\n**Tick**:\nOne mark in the row that draws a past Attempt: one per stimulus slot, placed at its authored `item_order` rather than its array position. Four states, because three would lie — `recorded`, `notJudged` (the judge never answered, so the mark is hollow), `silent` (she spoke nothing into that stimulus), `missing`.\n_Avoid_: Dot, pip, square, cell, bar\n\n**Target**:\nThe clock the section was meant to fit, taken from the assignment\'s time goal at her resolved accommodation. It measures elapsed time and nothing else, and a run is only ever compared against a Target equal to its own.\n_Avoid_: Goal (`within_goal` in the database also carries method conditions, which Target deliberately excludes), limit, par, deadline, pace\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 6.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '   1: // The Switchyard routes a conditional\'s two condition-groups onto two tracks:\r\n   2: // the sufficient clause onto IF, the necessary clause onto THEN. To animate\r\n   3: // that, the world needs the credited answer broken into its two clauses and\r\n   4: // each clause\'s polarity, which is what this module derives.\r\n   5: //\r\n   6: // The credited answers are authored, not free-form: every Conditional Rephrase\r\n   7: // item\'s first credited answer is written as "If X, then Y." The contrapositive\r\n   8: // is authored too, but several are written in shorthand ("Tank fine, pumps\r\n   9: // cycled."), so the flip is DERIVED from the parsed clauses and the authored\r\n  10: // text is shown alongside as the credited plate rather than parsed.\r\n  11: \r\n  12: export interface Clause {\r\n  13:   /** The clause text as authored, with any leading negation left intact. */\r\n  14:   text: string;\r\n  15:   /** True when the clause is stated negatively; drives the polarity lamp. */\r\n  16:   negated: boolean;\r\n  17: }\r\n  18: \r\n  19: export interface Routing {\r\n  20:   /** The sufficient condition. Couples onto the IF track. */\r\n  21:   ifClause: Clause;\r\n  22:   /** The necessary condition. Couples onto the THEN track. */\r\n  23:   thenClause: Clause;\r\n  24: }\r\n  25: \r\n  26: const IF_THEN = /^\\s*if\\s+(.+?),\\s*then\\s+(.+?)\\s*$/i;\r\n  27: \r\n  28: // Negation as it is actually written in the bank. Ordered longest-first so\r\n  29: // "cannot" is not matched by "not" with a different span.\r\n  30: const NEGATIONS = [\r\n  31:   "cannot",\r\n  32:   "can not",\r\n  33:   "does not",\r\n  34:   "did not",\r\n  35:   "do not",\r\n  36:   "will not",\r\n  37:   "would not",\r\n  38:   "is not",\r\n  39:   "are not",\r\n  40:   "was not",\r\n  41:   "were not",\r\n  42:   "may not",\r\n  43:   "must not",\r\n  44:   "no longer",\r\n  45:   "not ",\r\n  46:   "n\'t",\r\n  47:   "never",\r\n  48:   "neither",\r\n  49:   "nothing",\r\n  50: ];\r\n  51: \r\n  52: /** Whether a clause reads as negative, which lights the capsule\'s polarity ring. */\r\n  53: export function isNegated(clause: string): boolean {\r\n  54:   const s = ` ${clause.toLowerCase().trim()} `;\r\n  55:   if (/\\bno\\s+\\w/.test(s) && !/\\bno\\s+longer\\b/.test(s)) return true;\r\n  56:   return NEGATIONS.some((n) => s.includes(n));\r\n  57: }\r\n  58: \r\n  59: function trimTerminal(s: string): string {\r\n  60:   return s.replace(/\\s*[.;]\\s*$/, "").trim();\r\n  61: }\r\n  62: \r\n  63: /**\r\n  64:  * Parse an authored "If X, then Y." credited answer into its two clauses.\r\n  65:  * Returns null when the answer is not in that form, which the world treats as\r\n  66:  * "no routing available" and falls back to plain text rather than guessing.\r\n  67:  */\r\n  68: export function parseRouting(creditedAnswer: string): Routing | null {\r\n  69:   const match = trimTerminal(creditedAnswer).match(IF_THEN);\r\n  70:   if (!match) return null;\r\n  71: \r\n  72:   const [, sufficient, necessary] = match;\r\n  73:   const ifText = trimTerminal(sufficient);\r\n  74:   const thenText = trimTerminal(necessary);\r\n  75:   if (!ifText || !thenText) return null;\r\n  76: \r\n  77:   // A sentence carrying TWO conditionals is not two clauses, and the regex above\r\n  78:   // will happily hand back the whole tail as one. Item 62 parsed to a THEN of\r\n  79:   // "the flood layer dates to spring; and if the match is trustworthy, then the\r\n  80:   // stratum was sealed" -- an entire second rule inside one capsule, drawn on\r\n  81:   // screen as though it were a single condition. Returning null is the honest\r\n  82:   // answer: the world already falls back to the plain prompt and the authored\r\n  83:   // answers, which is correct, rather than a diagram that is wrong.\r\n  84:   if (/\\bif\\b|\\bthen\\b/i.test(ifText) || /\\bif\\b|\\bthen\\b/i.test(thenText)) return null;\r\n  85: \r\n  86:   return {\r\n  87:     ifClause: { text: ifText, negated: isNegated(ifText) },\r\n  88:     thenClause: { text: thenText, negated: isNegated(thenText) },\r\n  89:   };\r\n  90: }\r\n  91: \r\n  92: /**\r\n  93:  * The contrapositive as the machine performs it: the capsules swap tracks and\r\n  94:  * their polarity inverts, in one movement. This is derived rather than parsed\r\n  95:  * from the authored contrapositive, because the flip has to be a transformation\r\n  96:  * the student watches, not a second sentence that appears.\r\n  97:  */\r\n  98: export function flip(routing: Routing): Routing {\r\n  99:   return {\r\n 100:     ifClause: { text: routing.thenClause.text, negated: !routing.thenClause.negated },\r\n 101:     thenClause: { text: routing.ifClause.text, negated: !routing.ifClause.negated },\r\n 102:   };\r\n 103: }\r\n 104: \r\n 105: /**\r\n 106:  * The indicator word the item turns on ("only if", "unless", "the only").\r\n 107:  * Authored as the first quoted span of the item\'s explanation, which is where\r\n 108:  * the bank puts it. Returned so the departures board can light it in place,\r\n 109:  * and only after the yard has routed.\r\n 110:  */\r\n 111: export function indicatorOf(explanation: string): string | undefined {\r\n 112:   const m = explanation.match(/"([^"]{2,24})"/);\r\n 113:   return m ? m[1] : undefined;\r\n 114: }\r\n 115: \r\n 116: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\r\n 117:  * Signal faults\r\n 118:  *\r\n 119:  * The locked partial set is three named errors, and the spec gives each one its\r\n 120:  * own choreography in the yard. Which one a student made is DERIVED here by\r\n 120:  * own choreography in the yard. Two properties\r\n   7: // have to hold for that to be a teaching sequence rather than a pile: it climbs\r\n   8: // once, and it varies the indicator word and where the indicator sits, so no\r\n   9: // stretch of it drills the same word in the same seat.\r\n  10: //\r\n  11: // It held neither. Thirty-six items climbed a four-level ladder and the next\r\n  12: // twenty-six started again at the bottom, and the level labels ("Level 3: \'The\r\n  13: // only\' vs \'only,\' and the verb indicators") were drawn on screen as the\r\n  14: // student\'s Question Type chip. ];\r\n  25: \r\n  26: /** The indicator a Question Type names, e.g. "Requirement word: unless" -> "unless". */\r\n  27: function indicatorOf(category: string | undefined): string | null {\r\n  28: number {\r\n  34:   return prompt.trim().split(/\\s+/).length;\r\n  35: }\r\n  36: \r\n  37: function meanWords(slice: ActivityItem[]): number {\r\n  38:   return slice.reduce((n, i) => n + words(i.prompt), 0) / slice.length;\r\n  39: }\r\n  40: \r\n  41: describe("the Conditional Rephrase ladder", () => {\r\n  42:   it("opens on plainly marked single conditions", () => {\r\n  43:     for (const item of items.slice(0, 8)) {\r\n  44:       expect(item.category, `item ${item.order}`).toMatch(/^(Trigger|Requirement) word: /);\r\n  45:       // Nothing compound, contrasting or embedded this early.\r\n  46:       expect(item.prompt, `item ${item.order}`).not.toMatch(/\\bboth\\b|rather than/i);\r\n  47:     }\r\n  48:   });\r\n  49: \r\n  50:   it("keeps everything loaded to the last quarter", () => {\r\n  51:     const firstLoaded = items.findIndex((i) => LOADED.some((l) => i.category?.startsWith(l)));\r\n  52:     expect(firstLoaded).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(Math.floor(items.length * 0.75));\r\n  53:     // And it does not stop climbing before the end.\r\n  54:     expect(items[items.length - 1].category).toBe("Two conditionals in one sentence");\r\n  55:   });\r\n  56: \r\n  57:   it("gets longer and heavier as it climbs", () => {\r\n  58:     const q = Math.floor(items.length / 4);\r\n  59:     const first = meanWords(items.slice(0, q));\r\n  60:     const last = meanWords(items.slice(-q));\r\n  61:     expect(last).toBeGreaterThan(first * 1.3);\r\n  62:     // Monotone across );\r\n  66:     for (let n = 1; n < quarters.length; n += 1) {\r\n  67:       expect(quarters[n], `quarter ${n + 1} is not below quarter ${n}`).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(\r\n  68:         quarters[n - [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 27.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from "react";\r\nimport { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";\r\nimport { getActivity, getAssignmentById, getStudentAssignment, listStudentAssignments } from "../api/activitiesClient";\r\nimport type { Activity, ActivityAssignment, ActivityAssignmentSummary, ActivityStatus } from "../api/types";\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Shape an activity DEFINITION into the assignment the runner expects, without\r\n * writing anything. This is what a teacher preview runs on. Previews used to\r\n * call the real assign path against the teacher\'s own account, which wrote a\r\n * genuine `activity_assignments` row plus a Classroom item, so every activity\r\n * he opened to look at turned up under "Assigned to you" on his own Classroom\r\n * and stayed there. A preview is a read; it gets no row.\r\n */\r\nfunction previewAssignment(activity: Activity, userId: string): ActivityAssignment {\r\n  return {\r\n    id: `preview:${activity.id}`,\r\n    activity_id: activity.id,\r\n    student_id: userId,\r\n    title: activity.title,\r\n    subtitle: activity.subtitle,\r\n    instructions: activity.instructions,\r\n    item_format: activity.item_format,\r\n    /*\r\n     * Section mode is carried by three fields, and a preview that drops them\r\n     * silently runs the WRONG PLAYER. `ActivityRunner` picks the section runner\r\n     * on `mode === "section"`; without it the branch falls through to the v1\r\n     * chunk-by-chunk runner, which judges live, accepts typing and walks a\r\n     * cursor -- every one of which section mode forbids. The preview then shows\r\n     * a drill that has not existed since ADR 0012, and looks like a design\r\n     * failure rather than a missing field.\r\n     */\r\n    mode: activity.mode,\r\n    time_goal_minutes: activity.time_goal_minutes,\r\n    source_section: activity.source_section,\r\n    items: activity.items,\r\n    status: "assigned",\r\n    score: null,\r\n    started_at: null,\r\n    completed_at: null,\r\n    assigned_by: userId,\r\n    class_id: userId,\r\n    created_at: new Date().toISOString(),\r\n  };\r\n}\r\n\r\nconst STATUS_ORDER: Record<ActivityStatus, number> = {\r\n  in_progress: 0,\r\n  assigned: 1,\r\n  completed: 2,\r\n  abandoned: 3,\r\n};\r\n\r\n/** Student-side list of my activity assignments, sorted by status then recency. */\r\nexport function useStudentActivities() {\r\n  const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  const [assignments, setAssignments] = useState<ActivityAssignmentSummary[]>([]);\r\n  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);\r\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\r\n\r\n  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {\r\n    if (!user) return;\r\n    setLoading(true);\r\n    try {\r\n      const rows = await listStudentAssignments(user.id);\r\n      rows.sort((a, b) => {\r\n        const s = STATUS_ORDER[a.status] - STATUS_ORDER[b.status];\r\n        if (s !== 0) return s;\r\n        return new Date(b.created_at).getTime() - new Date(a.created_at).getTime();\r\n      });\r\n      setAssignments(rows);\r\n      setError(null);\r\n    } catch (e) {\r\n      console.error("[activity:assign:student:list] failed", { userId: user.id, error: e });\r\n      setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Failed to load activities");\r\n      setAssignments([]);\r\n    } finally {\r\n      setLoading(false);\r\n    }\r\n  }, [user]);\r\n\r\n  useEffect(() => {\r\n    if (user) void refresh();\r\n  }, [user, refresh]);\r\n\r\n  return { assignments, loading, error, refresh };\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** Student-side single activity assignment. */\r\nexport function useStudentActivity(\r\n  assignmentId: string | undefined,\r\n  previewActivityId?: string,\r\n) {\r\n  const { user } = useAuth();\r\n  const [assignment, setAssignment] = useState<ActivityAssignment | null>(null);\r\n  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);\r\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\r\n\r\n  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {\r\n    if (!user || (!assignmentId && !previewActivityId)) return;\r\n    setLoading(true);\r\n    try {\r\n      if (previewActivityId) {\r\n        const activity = await getActivity(previewActivityId);\r\n        setAssignment(activity ? previewAssignment(activity, user.id) : null);\r\n        setError(null);\r\n        return;\r\n      }\r\n      // The student-scoped read comes back empty both for an id that is not\r\n      // real and for one issued to somebody else. The second is the case where\r\n      // an admin is opening a link they handed out, so ask again by id alone\r\n      // and let RLS answer: an admin gets the row, a student gets the same\r\n      // null they already had. No client-side role check is involved, and the\r\n      // runner runs a foreign row read-only.\r\n      const row = await getStudentAssignment(assignmentId, user.id);\r\n      setAssignment(row ?? (await getAssignmentById(assignmentId)));\r\n      setError(null);\r\n    } catch (e) {\r\n      console.error("[activity:assign:student:get] failed", { userId: user.id, assignmentId, error: e });\r\n      setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Failed to load activity");\r\n      setAssignment(null);\r\n    } finally {\r\n      setLoading(false);\r\n    }\r\n  }, [user, assignmentId, previewActivityId]);\r\n\r\n  useEffect(() => {\r\n    void refresh();\r\n  }, [refresh]);\r\n\r\n  return { assignment, loading, error, refresh };\r\n}\r\n'}]

This\r\n   2: -- migration only repairs the lesson copy stored in Postgres: "otherwise" is\r\n   3: -- an if-not construction, not a biconditional. Active assignment snapshots\r\n   4: -- receive the same repair so a student never sees the stale contradiction.\r\n   5: \r\n   6: update public.activities\r\n   7: set instructions = jsonb_set(\r\n   8:       jsonb_set(\r\n   9:         instructions,\r\n  10:         \'{coach,rubric,7}\',\r\n  11:         to_jsonb(\'If and only if and then and only then are biconditionals, where the arrow genuinely runs both ways and the converse is therefore credited. Only on these items. A student may write both conditionals, or a single two-way statement, or say that the two sides stand or fall together. Do not credit an exclusive either-or, which says something different. Otherwise is not a biconditional; it means if the first alternative does not happen, the stated consequence follows.\'::text)\r\n  12:       ),\r\n  13:       \'{rules,9}\',\r\n  14:       to_jsonb(\'If and only if and then and only then make the arrow run both ways. Otherwise means if not; it does not make the converse valid.\'::text)\r\n  15:     )\r\n  16: where slug = \'conditional-rephrase\';\r\n  17: \r\n  18: alter table public.activity_assignments disable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;\r\n  19: \r\n  20: update public.activity_assignments assignment\r\n  21: set instructions = activity.instructions\r\n  22: from public.activities activity\r\n  23: where assignment.activity_id = activity.id\r\n  24:   and activity.slug = \'conditional-rephrase\'\r\n  25:   and assignment.status in (\'assigned\', \'in_progress\')\r\n  26:   and assignment.instructions is distinct from activity.instructions;\r\n  27: \r\n  28: alter table public.activity_assignments enable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': ' 130:  * RLS bug when the actual answer was "you are signed in as the wrong account".\r\n 131:  * That happens constantly to anyone holding both an admin and a student login.\r\n 132:  *\r\n 133:  * This probes by id alone and lets RLS answer. An admin can read every\r\n 134:  * assignment, so a `true` here means the row is real and owned by someone else.\r\n 135:  * A student reads only their own, so they get `false` and keep the plain\r\n 136:  * not-found wording, which for them is the truth. It never returns the row or\r\n 137:  * anything in it, only whether one exists.\r\n 138:  */\r\n 139: export async function assignmentBelongsToAnotherAccount(assignmentId: string): Promise<boolean> {\r\n 140:   // Every failure resolves to false rather than rejecting. This only ever\r\n 141:   // upgrades an error message, so a probe that cannot answer must leave the\r\n 142:   // plain wording alone instead of taking the screen down with it, or telling\r\n 143:   // a student to go hunting for an account that does not exist.\r\n 144:   try {\r\n 145:     const { data, error } = await supabase\r\n 146:       .from("activity_assignments")\r\n 147:       .select("id")\r\n 148:       .eq("id", assignmentId)\r\n 149:       .maybeSingle();\r\n 150:     if (error) return false;\r\n 151:     return Boolean(data);\r\n 152:   } catch {\r\n 153:     return false;\r\n 154:   }\r\n 155: }\r\n 156: \r\n 157: /** Student: mark an assignment in_progress on first open (assigned -> in_progress only). */\r\n 158: export async function markAssignmentStarted(assignmentId: string): Promise<void> {\r\n 159:   const { error } = await supabase\r\n 160:     .from("activity_assignments")\r\n 161:     .update({ status: "in_progress", started_at: new Date().toISOString() })\r\n 162:     .eq("id", assignmentId)\r\n 163:     .eq("status", "assigned");\r\n 164:   if (error) throw error;\r\n 165: }\r\n 166: \r\n 167: /**\r\n 168:  * PostgREST\'s two ways of saying "you named a column I do not have": the schema\r\n 169:  * cache miss on insert, and Postgres\'s own undefined_column. Either means the\r\n 170:  * telemetry migration has not been applied to this database yet.\r\n'}]

e.message : "Failed to load assignments");\r\n>       setAssignments([]);\r\n      } finally {\r\n        setLoading(false);\r\n      }\r\n    }, [studentId]);\r\n  \r\n      void refresh();\r\n    }, [refresh]);\r\n  \r\n>   return { assignments, loading, error, refresh };\r\n  }\r\n  \r\n> export interface AssignResult {\r\n    ok: boolean;\r\n    id?: string;\r\n    error?: string;\r\n    /** Whether the email leg succeeded (only meaningful when notify was requested). */\r\n    emailOk?: boolean;\r\n  }\r\n  \r\n  /**\r\n>  * Admin action: snapshot-assign an activity to a student. When `notify` is set\r\n>  * (the real assign, not a self-preview), it also drops the activity into the\r\n   * student\'s Classroom and fires the inbox + bell + email notification, mirroring\r\n>  * the homework assign flow. Notification failure never fails the assignment.\r\n   */\r\n> export function useAssignActivity() {\r\n    const { user } = useAuth();\r\n>   const [assigning, setAssigning] = useState(false);\r\n  \r\n>   const assign = useCallback(\r\n      async (\r\n        activity: Activity,\r\n        studentId: string,\r\n        opts?: { notify?: boolean },\r\n>     ): Promise<AssignResult> => {\r\n        if (!user) return { ok: false, error: "Not signed in" };\r\n>       setAssigning(true);\r\n        try {\r\n>         const { ok, failed } = await assignWork({\r\n            teacherId: user.id,\r\n            studentIds: [studentId],\r\n            item: { kind: "activity", activityId: activity.id },\r\n          });\r\n  \r\n          const result = ok[0];\r\n          if (!result) {\r\n>           return { ok: false, error: failed[0]?.error ?? "Assign failed" };\r\n          }\r\n  \r\n          // `notify: false` still writes the classroom item, which is what makes\r\n          // the activity reachable at all; it only suppresses reporting on the\r\n>         // messages. assignWork always notifies, so the flag now just decides\r\n          // how much detail comes back.\r\n>         if (!opts?.notify) return { ok: true, id: result.assignmentId };\r\n  \r\n          return {\r\n            ok: true,\r\n>           id: result.assignmentId,\r\n            emailOk: result.notify?.email_ok,\r\n            fullyNotified: Boolean(\r\n              result.notify && result.notify.inbox_ok && result.notify.bell_ok && result.notify.email_ok,\r\n            ),\r\n          };\r\n        } catch (e) {\r\n>         console.error("[activity:admin:assign] failed", { studentId, activityId: activity.id, error: e });\r\n>         return { ok: false, error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Assign failed" };\r\n        } finally {\r\n>         setAssigning(false);\r\n        }\r\n      },\r\n      [user],\r\n    );\r\n  \r\n>   return { assign, assigning };\r\n  }\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728213647_be46cd8c-f535-4e20-ad64-0096736cd6b3.sql:1:create or replace function public.mcp_assign_homework_set(\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728203000_mcp_admin_classroom_writes.sql:228:create or replace function public.mcp_assign_passage_map_homework(\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728203000_mcp_admin_classroom_writes.sql:339:create or replace function public.mcp_get_assignment_status(p_assignment_id uuid)\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728203000_mcp_admin_classroom_writes.sql:375:create or replace function public.guard_ta_assignment_immutables()\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728210000_mcp_assign_homework_set.sql:7:create or replace function public.mcp_assign_homework_set(\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728210058_c6d415e8-7366-42f3-8e07-124deabffb6e.sql:227:create or replace function public.mcp_assign_passage_map_homework(\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728210058_c6d415e8-7366-42f3-8e07-124deabffb6e.sql:338:create or replace function public.mcp_get_assignment_status(p_assignment_id uuid)\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728210058_c6d415e8-7366-42f3-8e07-124deabffb6e.sql:374:create or replace function public.guard_ta_assignment_immutables()\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728214214_a7ef2dd1-5911-4892-b84e-4587ba46c5e1.sql:132:create or replace function public.mcp_assign_passage_map_homework(\r\nsupabase/migrations\\20260728214214_a7ef2dd1-5911-4892-b84e-4587ba46c5e1.sql:235:create or replace function public.mcp_assign_homework_set(\r\n\r\n'}]

We replay the per-item state\r\n   6: // machine (correct | attempt cap | coach-failure null verdict) to find where\r\n   7: // the student actually stopped, what their resolving verdict was, and the text\r\n   8: // they last wrote. This drives "resume where you left off" and lets the final\r\n   9: // score count attempts across sessions.\r\n  10: \r\n  11: import { MAX_ATTEMPTS } from "./api/types";\r\n  12: import type { ActivityItem, ActivityItemResponse, Verdict } from "./api/types";\r\n  13: \r\n  14: export interface ReconstructedProgress {\r\n  15:   /** Resolving verdict per item order (correct, or the last attempt\'s verdict). */\r\n  16:   verdicts: Record<number, Verdict | null>;\r\n  17:   /** The text the student last submitted per item order, for the summary. */\r\n  18:   responses: Record<number, string>;\r\n  19:   /** Count of resolved items at the front; also the index to resume at. */\r\n  20:   resolvedCount: number;\r\n  21:   /**\r\n  22:    * Attempts already spent on the item they stopped ON (the first unresolved\r\n  23:    * one). Zero when that item was never touched. Resuming has to carry this:\r\n  24:    * restarting the counter at zero both hands back an attempt the student\r\n  25:    * already used and writes a second `attempt_no = 1` row, which makes the log\r\n  26:    * ambiguous for every later replay.\r\n  27:    */\r\n  28:   pendingAttempts: number;\r\n  29:   /** What they last wrote on that item, so a half-finished answer survives a reload. */\r\n  30:   pendingText: string;\r\n  31: }\r\n  32: \r\n  33: /**\r\n  34:  * Replay an attempt log against the ordered item list. `items` must be sorted\r\n  35:  * by `order`; `responses` may be in any order (we group by item_order and read\r\n  36:  * the last attempt by attempt_no).\r\n  37:  */\r\n  38: export function reconstructProgress(\r\n  39:   items: ActivityItem[],\r\n  40:   responses: ActivityItemResponse[],\r\n  41: ): ReconstructedProgress {\r\n  42:   const byItem = new Map<number, ActivityItemResponse[]>();\r\n  43:   for (const r of responses) {\r\n  44:     const list = byItem.get(r.item_order);\r\n  45:     if (list) list.push(r);\r\n  46:     else byItem.set(r.item_order, [r]);\r\n  47:   }\r\n  48:   for (const list of byItem.values()) {\r\n  49:     list.sort((a, b) => a.attempt_no - b.attempt_no);\r\n  50:   }\r\n  51: \r\n  52:   const verdicts: Record<number, Verdict | null> = {};\r\n  53:   const text: Record<number, string> = {};\r\n  54:   let resolvedCount = 0;\r\n  55:   let pendingAttempts = 0;\r\n  56:   let pendingText = "";\r\n  57: \r\n  58:   for (const item of items) {\r\n  59:     const rows = byItem.get(item.order);\r\n  60:     if (!rows || rows.length === 0) break; // first untouched item: stop here\r\n  61: \r\n  62:     const correct = rows.find((r) => r.verdict === "correct");\r\n  63:     const last = rows[rows.length - 1];\r\n  64:     const hitCap = last.attempt_no >= MAX_ATTEMPTS;\r\n  65:     const coachFailed = rows.some((r) => r.verdict === null);\r\n  66:     const resolved = Boolean(correct) || hitCap || coachFailed;\r\n  67: \r\n  68:     // One sub-cap, non-correct attempt means they were mid-retry. The item is\r\n  69:     // NOT resolved, so the run resumes here, but the attempt they already spent\r\n  70:     // and the words they already wrote come back with it.\r\n  71:     if (!resolved) {\r\n  72:       pendingAttempts = last.attempt_no;\r\n  73:       pendingText = last.response_text;\r\n  74:       break;\r\n  75:     }\r\n  76: \r\n  77:     const resolver = correct ?? last;\r\n  78:     verdicts[item.order] = correct ? "correct" : last.verdict;\r\n  79:     text[item.order] = resolver.response_text;\r\n  80:     resolvedCount += 1;\r\n  81:   }\r\n  82: \r\n  83:   return { verdicts, responses: text, resolvedCount, pendingAttempts, pendingText };\r\n  84: }\r\n  85: \r\n  86: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\r\n  87:  * Scoring\r\n  88:  *\r\n  89:  * A null verdict means the Coach could not be reached. It is not a wrong\r\n  90:  * answer, and it used to be scored as one: the weighted sum divided by the item\r\n  91:  * COUNT, so an item nobody judged was arithmetically identical to an item the\r\n  92:  * student got wrong. A student whose network dropped on question nine was\r\n  93:  * marked down for it, permanently.\r\n  94:  *\r\n  95:  * The denominator is now the number of items that actually got a verdict, and\r\n  96:  * the parts are carried separately so the screen can say which denominator it\r\n  97:  * used rather than presenting a percentage and hoping.\r\n  98:  * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\r\n  99: \r\n 100: const VERDICT_WEIGHT: Record<Verdict, number> = { correct: 1, partial: 0.5, incorrect: 0 };\r\n 101: \r\n 102: export interface RunScore {\r\n 103:   /** Weighted sum over the judged items. */\r\n 104:   numerator: number;\r\n 105:   /** How many items reached a verdict, right or wrong. */\r\n 106:   judged: number;\r\n 107:   /** How many items the run contained. */\r\n 108:   total: number;\r\n 109:   /**\r\n 110:    * Percentage over the judged items, or null when nothing was judged at all.\r\n 111:    * Null is the honest answer there: returning 0 would repeat the false\r\n 112:    * judgement this function exists to remove, and it renders as "not scored".\r\n 113:    */\r\n 114:   percent: number | null;\r\n 115: }\r\n 116: \r\n 117: /** The run\'s score, with the denominator it was computed over kept in view. */\r\n 118: export function scoreFor(verdicts: Record<number, Verdict | null>, total: number): RunScore {\r\n 119:   let numerator = 0;\r\n 120:   let judged = 0;\r\n 121:   for (const verdict of Object.values(verdicts)) {\r\n 122:     if (!verdict) continue; // the Coach never answered: not a zero, not counted\r\n 123:     numerator += VERDICT_WEIGHT[verdict] ?? 0;\r\n 124:     judged += 1;\r\n 125:   }\r\n 126:   return {\r\n 127:     numerator,\r\n 128:     judged,\r\n 129:     total,\r\n 130:     percent: judged === 0 ? null : Math.round((numerator / judged) * 100),\r\n 131:   };\r\n 132: }\r\n'}]

A Coach outage silently costs the student marks, and calls a right answer wrong — `ActivityItemView.tsx:78` and `ActivityRunner.tsx:28`\r\ndocs/audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md:82:`scoreFor` weights `null` as 0 and divides by the full item count, so every item lost to an outage is a permanent zero on the graded score, with no path back — the item is resolved and the run moves on.\r\ndocs/audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md:84:This runs against the truthfulness rule: the number no longer traces to anything the student did. **Fix:** a distinct "could not check this one" state (neutral, not red), exclude un-judged items from the denominator, and offer a re-judge on the next open. The only `role="status"` in the feature is the Switchyard\'s fault line.\r\ndocs/audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md:117:- **The credited answer sits in the DOM before it is revealed**, veiled by CSS only. A screen reader will read it out. It should not be rendered until revealed.\r\ndocs/audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md:146:1. **Judge the obvious answers locally, in under 50 ms.** `routing.ts` already parses "If X, then Y" and `diagnose()` already names the three canonical errors (reversal, dropped negation, trimmed condition). When a student\'s parse matches the credited parse, that is a deterministic correct — no model call, no wait, no cost, and no chance of the model contradicting the answer key. Send only the near-misses to the Coach, which is where prose feedback actually earns its latency. This is the biggest single quality-and-speed move available.\r\ndocs/audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md:154:9. **Stream the Coach feedback.** Same total time, much shorter felt time.\r\ndocs/audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md:161:- **All item content**, per Joshua\'s instruction: prompts, credited answers, explanations, Question Types, ladder order.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:4:**Source:** [conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md](../audits/conditional-rephrase-activity-audit-2026-08-22.md) — graded **72 / 100**\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:8:**Frozen, by Joshua\'s instruction:** all item content. Stage 11 is the slot it lands in.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:18:Audit finding 9, last bullet: *"The credited answer sits in the DOM before it is revealed, veiled by CSS only. A screen reader will read it out."*\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:20:That is not true. `SwitchyardStage.tsx:468` sets `aria-hidden={active ? undefined : true}` on the inactive plate, and `switchyard.css:416` applies `filter: blur(7px)` to the plate\'s text while it is inactive. The credited answer is hidden from assistive technology and unreadable visually. **No work is planned against this bullet.** The other four bullets of finding 9 stand and are Stage 6.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:28:| Items whose **first** credited answer parses as `If X, then Y` | **62 / 62** |\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:29:| Items whose **second** credited answer (the contrapositive) starts `If X, then Y` | **13 / 62** |\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:30:| Items whose second credited answer starts `If X, …` (comma, no "then") | **36 / 62** |\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:31:| Items whose second credited answer is shorthand or chained prose | **26 / 62** |\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:39:v1 of this plan argued the local judge outranked it. **After review, that claim is narrowed.** The exact-match fast path (Stage 2a) is still the biggest *performance* win and it does make the model unable to contradict the answer key **on the fast path** — but only there, and it no longer closes finding 4 by itself, because the classifier that would have done that is on probation. The honest ranking is now: Stage 1 is the biggest measured win, Stage 4 is the biggest teaching win, Stage 2 is the biggest felt-speed win and the only stage that produces evidence for a future decision.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:43:`CoachFeedback.tsx:49` gates the whole reveal block, including `item.explanation`, on `revealed`. `revealed` is `true` only when the student ran out of attempts or pressed Show answer (`ActivityItemView.tsx:88`, `:99`, `:125`). On a **correct** answer, `resolve("correct", false)` — `revealed` is false.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:47:### New finding B — the second credited answer is labelled wrong\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:49:`CoachFeedback.tsx:59` renders `item.credited_answers.slice(1)` under the label **"Also accepted:"**. Stage 2\'s fast path is built on this and credits any authored direction.
