That edit is content and is Joshua\'s; it is the one line Stage 11 must not miss.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:64:- `instructions.coach.rubric[0]`: *"Either direction is credited. … a student who supplies only one of them has still translated correctly."* — either alone is enough.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:66:Today the Coach follows the rubric, so a student who ignores the second half of the instruction is marked correct. Stage 2\'s fast path has to know which of these is the rule, and Stage 4\'s flip changes what the yard is claiming. **This is a decision, not a defect, and it is Joshua\'s.** See §5.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:70:### Correction 4 [v2] — Stage 2\'s original design would have graded correct answers wrong\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:75:straight = alignment(student.if, credited.if) + alignment(student.then, credited.then) = 0 + 0    = 0\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:76:crossed  = alignment(student.if, credited.then) + alignment(student.then, credited.if) = 0.8 + 0.75 = 1.55\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:80:A student who types `credited_answers[1]` verbatim would have been graded **partial: reversal**. A nonsense answer graded **partial**.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:90:`parseRouting` is a **display parser for one authored format**, not a semantic grader. It requires the comma and the literal word "then". The rubric explicitly permits free wording and either direction. Stage 2 is rewritten around that.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:124:`activity_item_responses` stores verdict, response text and attempt number, and nothing else. There is no judge source, parser version, fault, latency or fallback reason. A local judge cannot be validated, compared, or rolled back from production data without those columns. Stage 2 adds them before it needs them.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:133:- **Repair** — the eleven audit findings, minus the one that was wrong, plus the two new ones. This is what gets the grade from 72 to roughly 92.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:134:- **Amplify** — six upgrades that are not fixes for anything. This is the last eight points, and it is where the drill stops being a well-built form and starts being the thing that teaches.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:142:| 2 | The fast path, and the judge on probation | Both | `[engine]` | 5 (part), F, sc#1 | M |\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:250:## Stage 2 — The fast path, and the judge on probation `[engine]` **[v2 — redesigned]**\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:256:v1 proposed making `diagnose()` authoritative for the graded score. Correction 4 shows what that does: a student typing the credited contrapositive gets **partial: reversal**, and a nonsense answer on item 18 gets **partial: dropped-negation**. `parseRouting` is a display parser for one authored format; the rubric permits free wording and either direction. Making it the grader would have shipped the exact truthfulness violation the stage claimed to fix.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:260:### 2a — The exact-match fast path *(authoritative, safe)*\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:262:Parse **every** credited answer for the item, not just the first. So a change to `task`, `rules` or `coach.rubric` would update the `activities` row and leave every existing unstarted assignment snapshot carrying the **old instructions** — including the old coach rubric, which is what actually grades the student. Any content migration that touches `instructions` has to re-snapshot that column too, or the fix reaches new assignments only and silently misses the ones already issued.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:590:- **Re-drill the misses.** The end screen offers the items they missed as an ungraded practice run that writes nothing. The fast path cannot be built without the answer. My recommendation: **either direction is credited, both are praised** — it matches the rubric that has been grading students all along, and it is the reading under which items 46, 47 and 48 make sense.\r\ndocs/plans/2026-08-22-conditional-rephrase-build-plan.md:695:5. **Which stages, in what order?** My recommendation if the answer is "some of it": **0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 7.** That is the migration, the largest measured performance win, the fast path plus the telemetry that makes a future judging decision evidence-based, the change that stops a right answer being scored wrong, the change that makes the drill teach the thing it is named for, and the highest teaching gain per line in the document. Stages 5, 6, 8, 9, 10 are quality a good product has and no student will name. Stage 12 is the one that gives you something back.\r\n'}]

## assistant

Trace complete.

- The no-AI redesign is already on `origin/main` in commit `baa42fa`.
- Conditional Rephrase now uses an authoritative browser-side `localJudge`, bypassing the AI call for every verdict.
- Existing focused tests pass: 28/28.
- Main remaining risk: 49 of 62 contrapositives are not authored in the strict parser format, so valid normalized contrapositives can be misgraded.
- Safest zero-AI redesign is structured clause/polarity input. Minimal hardening is to parse authored contrapositives with the existing loose parser and expand whole-bank adversarial tests.
